## 0t. THE HEADLINE FINDING WAS WRONG ON THE FIGURE AND RIGHT ON THE YEAR, AND THE FIGURE WAS THE PART IT SPENT ITS EVIDENCE ON

**THE REVIEW SAID: *the shutter anchor `2,078` is wrong by exactly 360 days, and is now load-bearing in six places*. IT PROPOSED `1,718` AND THREE NEW INTERVALS.**

**`2,078` IS THE NINTH OF NOVEMBER OF YEAR SIX AND IT IS CORRECT, AND THE SHUTTER DID GO BARE THAT DAY.** `1,718` IS THE NINTH OF NOVEMBER OF YEAR FIVE AND IT IS A DIFFERENT EVENT — the day he wrote the sentences at the foot of the standing page in his own hand. The two are a year apart, both are on the page, and eleven finished footers measure from the second one.

**`chapter-0645.md`:195 NAMES BOTH IN ONE PARAGRAPH — the interval from year five, then "on the ninth of November of this year" for the taking, in a chapter dated thirty days later. A FINDING THAT READS THAT FOOTER CANNOT MISS TWO EVENTS.**

**AND `chapter-0650.md`'S BODY IS NOT IN CONTRADICTION WITH ITS OWN FOOTER, WHICH THE REVIEW SAID IT WAS.** The body says fifty-one days and the footer says four hundred and eleven, and they measure from different anchors: `2,129 − 2,078 = 51` and `2,129 − 1,718 = 411`. The same woman standing in front of something from the thirtieth of September (`2,039`) and then in front of nothing from the ninth (`2,078`) is thirty-nine days and then fifty-one, which is a clean account and not a contradiction. **THE CHAPTER IS CORRECT AND WAS NOT TOUCHED, AND `state/current.md`'s repair of `:47` and `:55` FROM *thirty-eight days* TO *fifty-one days* WAS CORRECT AND STANDS. A LATER PASS MUST NOT "FIX" IT BACK.**

**WHAT WAS ACTUALLY WRONG, AND IT WAS WORTH THE FINDING: FOUR FILES LABELLED THE SAME CORRECT FIGURE WITH TWO DIFFERENT YEARS.** `outline/volume-14.md` §2b said year six and §5b said **year five** with the same `2,078`; `state/continuity.md` §0b and `state/current.md` §0a both said year five. **THE FIGURE WAS NEVER IN DOUBT AND ONLY THE LABEL WAS WRONG, WHICH IS THE CHEAPER WAY TO BE WRONG BECAUSE THE SUBTRACTION STILL WORKS AND NOTHING LOOKS BROKEN.** All four now say year six.

**AND THE HAZARD THE FINDING SURFACED BY ACCIDENT IS NOW NAMED IN THREE FILES AND IS REAL.** `2,489 − 2,078 = 411` at 700, and `chapter-0650.md`:193 already prints four hundred and eleven days for `2,129 − 1,718`. **THE SAME NUMERAL, TWO ANCHORS, TWO CHAPTERS. ## 0z. THE FOUR STANDING BODY DECISIONS, AND EACH ONE EXISTS BECAUSE SOMEBODY BORROWED SOMEBODY ELSE

**1 · NOBODY PUTS A HAND FLAT ON ANYTHING IN 651–700.** Mavis Rooke's hand-flat was hers at `chapter-0547.md`:86 and was put on Ines Duarte at `chapter-0584.md`:75 where it was a borrow, and the gate row that certified the absence was false. **PERLA QUIST'S HANDS ARE HER OWN AND HER PROXIMITY TO A LIST IS THE WHOLE OF HER, AND NOBODY ELSE PUTS THEM FLAT ON A TABLE.**
**2 · PERLA QUIST HAS NO MARK AND NO AGE.** A woman of about fifty, because a woman of about fifty is what the page already has in that shape — **AND SHE IS NOT THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT THE CORNER, WHO CANNOT WRITE HER OWN NAME AND IS A SEPARATE PERSON ON THE PAGE AT 648 AND 650. A BATCH THAT MERGES THE TWO IS WRONG AND THE CLOSE AUDIT ALREADY REPAIRED THIS EXACT COLLISION ONCE.**
**3 · THE BOY IS NOT AGED.** Six announced-age strings survive at 639 and 640 — *I am sixteen* twice and *You are sixteen* once and *a boy of sixteen* once at 639, and *a boy of sixteen* twice at 640 — handed forward as a disclosed exception and never closed. A chapter that needs one says *the boy* and stops.
**4 · THE WOMAN WITH THE SHORT-HANDLED BROOM IS NOT NAMED AND NOT ASKED.** She cleans the four inches every morning because they are under the wall and nobody spits there, and she said once, unasked, that she has never once seen anybody put anything on them. **Volume 14 is the volume in which the temptation to ask her is strongest, and it is refused.**

## 0c. WHAT 601–650 SETTLE, AND WHAT IS STILL OPEN

**SETTLED AND NOT TO BE RE-DERIVED BY A LATER CHAPTER:**

- **The fourth of his four conditions is off the office's own standing page, in the office's own hand, with his name and the date under the striking, and nothing in the two inches where it was.** It cannot be restored.
- **What the fourth line was carrying was the claims of people who cannot read.**
- **The yard took it off its own board on the seventh of July with a rag and a bucket in about eleven minutes, and came back for the other three on the ninth of November and did it in about nine hours, and nobody asked him.**
- **The woman two flights up off Cordwain learned, at 617, from a slate and from a voice, and was not thanked and was given nothing. §8's one permitted close is closed and stays closed.**
- **An auditor who certifies an hour puts an hour in the city that nobody can take back, because the sentence under every hour says any number in this city may be one somebody invented. The only hour she gave him is four o'clock.**
- **A fortieth is the smallest share she can lift and it does not stay behind in the person. The board has eleven columns, a heavy line across the foot of every one, and nothing under any of them, and no line anywhere on it for going up.**
- **Making good is render. The bottom course is dressed stone and does not come off. About sixty-two of the month's forms are stacked inside that wall, two empty squares on every one.**

**STILL OPEN, AND NOT THE CLOSE'S TO SHUT:** the figure of the account · the reach of the return · whether the fourth condition was ever true of anybody's deed · who put the white tile up · **who cut the two words, which is a question on a piece of paper in six years and no answer on any page** · the keeper's six words · the year the composition of premises 34-6-118 began · his brother's file · **Ines Duarte's four questions, which stand open, are named as open at 643, and are answered nowhere in this volume and may not be closed by the close** · the anniversary at 2,047 · **and the wall, which §8 PERMITS VOLUME 13 TO OPEN AND WHICH 608, 639, 640, 645, 648, 649 AND 650 ALL CARRY AND NONE OF THEM CLOSES.**

## 0r. THE TWO ARITHMETIC BREAKS THE WRITER'S OWN RE-DERIVATION CAUGHT, AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT EACH

**NINETY-TWO INTERVALS ANY OF THE TEN FOOTERS PRINTS WERE RE-DERIVED FROM DAY NUMBERS ONE AT A TIME AFTER THE DUPLICATION REPAIRS. NINETY RE-DERIVE EXACTLY. TWO DID NOT, AND BOTH ARE REPAIRED.**

1. **`chapter-0643.md` PRINTED *FORTY-FIVE DAYS* FOR THE GAP TO THE TWENTY-FOURTH SHEET.** `2,129 − 2,084 = 45` is **arithmetically correct** and the chapter also needed `2,114 − 2,084 = 30`, the gap to the fifteenth of December, because that is the chapter in front of it. **The figure was not wrong and the sentence around it was**, and a figure nobody re-reads in context is a figure that is wrong. **Repaired to thirty days**, with both operands beside it, and the forty-five stays at `state/current.md` §0a because a later reader will want to know it was ever printed.
2. **`chapter-0650.md` PRINTED *THE NEXT YEAR OPENS IN TWO DAYS*.** The last day of a year is the thirtieth of December and the next one opens on the first of February, which is day **2,160** and is **31 days** away, not two. **Repaired**, with the operand and the reason, and with the observation that **1 February of year seven is exactly six times three hundred and sixty and is the first exact figure this office has ever printed** — which belongs in a volume close and not on the last page of a chapter.

## 0r. WHAT 641–650 SETTLE, AND WHAT IS STILL OPEN

**SETTLED BY 641–650 AND NOT TO BE RE-DERIVED BY A LATER CHAPTER:**

- **THE FOURTH OF HIS FOUR CONDITIONS IS OFF THE OFFICE'S OWN STANDING PAGE, IN THE OFFICE'S OWN HAND, WITH HIS NAME AND THE DATE UNDER IT, AND NOTHING IN ITS PLACE.** It cannot be restored. A clerk enters what she is given and cannot improve it; an entry can be made and an entry cannot be taken back; the page goes out at four on the thirtieth and about nine people a week will ever look at it. **THE HEAD OF THE SHEET IS EIGHT HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO AND DOES NOT MOVE, AND THE SECOND COLUMN HAS TWO LINES IN IT AT 601 AND TWO IN IT AT 650, BECAUSE WHAT WENT ON AT 644 WENT ON AT THE FOOT.**
- **WHAT THE FOURTH LINE WAS CARRYING WAS THE CLAIMS OF PEOPLE WHO CANNOT READ**, and killing it means a person who cannot read a lease, a demand, a finding or a sheet can now say almost anything and cannot say that anybody took anything off her.
- **A FORTIETH IS THE SMALLEST SHARE SHE CAN LIFT AND IT DOES NOT STAY BEHIND IN THE PERSON.** The board has eleven columns and a heavy line across the foot of every one of them and **no line anywhere on it for going up**, and there has not been one since the first of February of the first year.
- **AN AUDITOR WHO CERTIFIES AN HOUR PUTS AN HOUR IN THE CITY THAT NOBODY CAN TAKE BACK, BECAUSE THE SENTENCE UNDER EVERY HOUR SAYS THAT ANY NUMBER IN THIS CITY MAY BE ONE SOMEBODY INVENTED** — and the only hour she gave him is four o'clock, which is not contested.
- **INES DUARTE HAS READ THE STANDING PAGE ALOUD TO ONE PERSON, IN A ROOM WITH A SHUT DOOR IN IT, ONCE A YEAR IN THE ELEVENTH MONTH, FOR ABOUT NINE YEARS, AND IT IS NOT AN OFFICE AND IT IS NOT IN ANY BOOK.** She has not said it to anybody and has asked him not to.
- **THE THREE THAT ARE LEFT OF THE FOUR CONDITIONS ARE STILL THE ONLY CHECKABLE ONES AND THEY STILL END ON THE THIRD**, and what came off the shutter on the ninth of November was not removed by him and was not his to keep.
- **THE WALL.** Making good is render. The bottom course is dressed stone and does not come off. **There are about sixty-two of the month's forms stacked inside that wall, one a month since the eighth of October of the first year, every one of them with two empty squares at the foot.** The painting is a Bureau contract in a different building and the two offices have not sent a paper to each other in nine years and there is nothing that makes them. **THE FIRST RUBBING EVER MADE THERE IS ON THE BACK OF A MONTH'S FORM AND IT IS IN THE INSIDE OF A COAT.**
- **A NUMBER ON A SLIP IS NOT A WAY OF GETTING ANYTHING AND IS A WAY OF BEING FOUND**, and he has one, and there is no column on the works schedule about two words and there never has been.

**STILL OPEN AND NOT TOUCHED BY 641–650:** the figure of the account · the reach of the return · whether the fourth condition was ever true of anybody's deed · who put the white tile up · **who cut the two words into the stone, which is a question on a piece of paper in six years and no answer on any page** · the keeper's six words · the year the composition of premises 34-6-118 began · his brother's file · **Ines Duarte's four questions, which stand open, are named as open at 643, and are answered nowhere in this volume and may not be closed by the close** · the anniversary at 2,047 · **and the wall itself, which §8 PERMITS VOLUME 13 TO OPEN AND WHICH 645, 648, 649 AND 650 ALL CARRY AND NONE OF THEM CLOSES: the form for the date the work was done goes in on the twenty-seventh of December under a number, the render comes off in the spring, the bottom course does not, a boy says he will be painting that corner in the ninth month of next year, and there is nothing on any form in this city about four inches of dressed stone.**

## 0r. THE ONE ARITHMETIC BREAK THE WRITER'S OWN RE-DERIVATION CAUGHT, AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT IT

**EVERY INTERVAL ANY OF THE TEN FOOTERS PRINTS WAS RE-DERIVED FROM DAY NUMBERS AGAIN, ONE AT A TIME, AFTER THE DUPLICATION REPAIRS. SIXTY-ONE INTERVALS WERE CHECKED. ONE FAILED.**

**`chapter-0634.md` PRINTED *TWENTY-ONE DAYS* FOR THE INTERVAL FROM THE ELEVENTH OF AUGUST.** The eleventh of August of year six is day **1,990** and the first of September is day **2,010**, and `2,010 − 1,990 = 20`. **The figure was a day out and it was a day out in a chapter whose whole argument is that a number nobody recomputes is a number that is wrong, which is the reason it is recorded here rather than quietly corrected.** Repaired to **twenty days**, with the same operands beside it.

**AND THE SAME PASS FOUND A DEVICE MISAPPLIED IN THE SAME CHAPTER.** The chapter's own opening sentence had said *twenty-three less the first of September borrows seven days of the thirtieth of August and leaves eight*, which is a borrow of seven days inside a sentence that is describing a borrow of twenty-two. **A month runs thirty days and stops, so there are seven days left in August after the twenty-third and the first of September is the day after the thirtieth, which makes eight days in all** — which is the right sentence, states the true number, and does not misname the month the days come off. **The same defect is now printed as a standing warning in `workspace/volume-13/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`: the month borrow only goes one way, and going forward past a month's end borrows nothing.**

**THE OTHER SIXTY INTERVALS RE-DERIVE EXACTLY AND ARE PRINTED WITH THEIR OPERANDS AT §0a. NOTHING ELSE WAS CHANGED.**

- **A COUNTER FIGURE CAN BE ADDED TO AND CANNOT BE TAKEN FROM**, and the rule is older than the Register and in nobody's hand, and no page in this city can be sent back to the division that asked it a question. 635 is the chapter.

## 0r. THE FOUR ARITHMETIC BREAKS THE REVIEW FOUND, AND WHAT EACH ONE NOW READS

**EVERY ONE OF THESE WAS RE-DERIVED FROM DAY NUMBERS BEFORE IT WAS REPAIRED AND AGAIN AFTER, AND NO REPAIR WAS MADE BY ADJUSTING A RESULT UNTIL IT AGREED WITH A PRINTED OPERAND.**

1. **`chapter-0622.md` SAID NINE DAYS WHERE IT IS EIGHT.** `1,925 − 1,917 = 8`, and 623 opens *"Eight days had gone since…"*. **Repaired in two places**, because the body carried the error as well as the footer: *in nine days he was going to be in a room* → **eight**, and *is nine days* → **eight days**, with *There is a week in between* corrected to **a week and a day** because eight is more than seven and the sentence was false in the only direction it could be.
2. **`chapter-0628.md` PRINTED 1,722 FOR THE NINTH OF NOVEMBER.** The prompt's anchor list has `1,722 = 13 Nov y5`, so the numeral and the date were four days apart and the interval was four days out. **1,718** re-derives: year five opens at 1,410 and the ninth of November stands `10 × 30 + 8 = 308` days after it, and `1,410 + 308 = 1,718`. The interval is then `1,964 − 1,718 = 246`, so **two hundred and forty-two became two hundred and forty-six.** **The clause after it was separately wrong**: it said *the first of the two lines went out at four on that day with a name under it*, which cannot be true, because the first of the two lines in the second column is his own and was entered on the fifteenth of October of the fourth year, and the second came in on the first of May of the fifth year. **It now says what happened on the ninth of November of the fifth year**, which is what 623, 626 and 628 all say it is.
3. **`chapter-0630.md` PRINTED TWO OPERANDS THAT CANCELLED BY ACCIDENT.** *Three times three hundred and sixty, which is one thousand and eighty* — year four opens at **1,050**, which is `1,770 − 2 × 360`. *Plus the two hundred and forty days into the year, plus fourteen* — the fifteenth of October stands `9 × 30 + 14 = 284` days after the first of January, which is **284** and not 240. `1,050 + 284 = 1,334`, and the total was right and the two terms were not. **Both are now printed with their derivation, which is the whole point of the device.**
4. **`chapter-0627.md` MISAPPLIED THE MONTH BORROW.** *Seven less the thirtieth of June comes out at seven with twenty-three borrowed off it* — nothing is borrowed going forward off a month's last day; the device is only valid backwards, as 623 uses it correctly. **It now reads: a month runs thirty days and stops, so a day seven after the thirtieth of June is the seventh of July, and there is no thirty-first to carry it.**

**SETTLED BY 621–630 AND NOT TO BE RE-DERIVED BY A LATER CHAPTER:**

- **THE INVENTED-FIGURES SENTENCE WAS ENTERED ON THE NINTH OF NOVEMBER OF THE FIFTH YEAR, WHICH IS DAY 1,718.** 623 dates it to *the November of the fifth year*; 626 dates the first print carrying it to the thirtieth of December of the fifth year; 628 and 630 now carry 1,718. **IT HAS GONE OUT AT FOUR ON THE THIRTIETH EVERY QUARTER SINCE AND HAS GONE OUT THREE TIMES — the thirtieth of December of the fifth year, the thirtieth of March of the sixth, and the thirtieth of June of the sixth. IT WILL GO OUT A FOURTH TIME AT 2,039.**
- **THE COLUMN HEADED CARRIED BY WAS RULED ON THE NINTH OF MARCH OF THE THIRD YEAR, WHICH IS DAY 758, AND LIN HAO'S OWN LINE WENT INTO IT ON THE FIFTEENTH OF OCTOBER OF THE FOURTH YEAR, WHICH IS DAY 1,334.** The second line came in through a counter on the first of May of the fifth year. **NO YEAR IS PRINTED FOR ANY OF THE THREE IN 601–650 AND NONE MAY BE.**
- **THE OFFICE'S AGE AT THE TWENTY-SECOND SHEET IS SIXTY-FOUR MONTHS AND TWENTY-NINE DAYS. THE LANE'S FIRST MORNING IS DAY −118 AND ITS MORNINGS AT 1,949 ARE 2,068. BOTH ARE SPENT FOR THE VOLUME AND 631–640 MAY NOT PRINT EITHER AGAIN.**
- **THE CHAPLAIN'S STANDING IS ABOUT FIFTEEN MONTHS AS AT 629, NOT ELEVEN YEARS AND NOT SIXTEEN MONTHS.** 620 says only *for years*. The correction is canon and the phrase is *since the spring of last year*. **NO CHAPTER OF 601–650 MAY GIVE HER A SPAN IN YEARS.**
- **ABOUT ELEVEN HUNDRED PEOPLE IN THE YARD HEARD LIN HAO SAY OUT LOUD TWICE THAT HE WAS THE ONLY PERSON ANYBODY COULD BE ASKED ABOUT. THAT NUMBER IS A COUNT OF HEARERS AND NOT A FIGURE ON THE SHEET AND IT IS NOT ONE OF THE FOUR FOUR-HUNDREDS.**

**STILL OPEN AND NOT TOUCHED BY 621–630:** the figure of the account · the reach of the return · whether the fourth condition was ever true of anybody's deed · who put the white tile up · the keeper's six words · the year the composition of premises 34-6-118 began · his brother's file · **and the anniversary at 2,047, which falls between 638 and 639 and which no chapter may name.**

All five were re-cut from disk by `sed -n` and in whitespace split and the two methods agree.**

**THE AXIS BROKEN IS THE ORDER**, carried by her room's rule and named by nobody: a folder is taken in the order it was opened and may not be taken out of order afterwards, and the first question in it is not the one anybody came for. **The chapter's structural difference from every other scene in this book: at 603 a woman had the authority and he was the one who could not be found; at 613 the room has the order and the order is hers, and the thing she has wanted for four years is to be asked, and he asks her what she is doing at the far table and she answers inside two seconds, and the chapter ends with her asking him the question she has wanted to put to anybody.** No clock inside the scene and no hour counted in it. No kettle, no chart, no arm turned up. **No hand flat on a table and no hand flat on anything.**

**AND THE NEXT FOUR SCENES OF THIS VOLUME ARE NOT IN THIS BATCH.** They are at **623** (Marguerite Vale, ninety-eight chapters, a real book and a carrier), **633** (Wren Kallo, five hundred and fifteen, on a returns desk and not in her rooms) and **643** (Ines Duarte, fifty-nine, and an hour she declines to give). **No chapter of 611–620 contains an explicit passage except 613, and 613 does not turn into one twice.**

## 0c. WHAT IS CARRIED OUT OF 611–620 AND INTO 621–630

- **A finding with his name in it**, drawn on the eleventh of March and up on the twenty-seventh, with **a copy of the standing page behind it** so that a magistrate turns it over on any morning.
- **A minute of a magistrate's clerk's own, in that file, going in front of a magistrate**, which records that the fourth of four lines is a statement about the absence of a page and that the rule relied on cannot be tried by the court or by any party. **It is the only page in that file nobody wrote but him, and there is no instrument in this city that takes it out.**
- **A sentence he wrote in nine files on nine counters in nine divisions**, in nine hands, one of them a division officer's own, and he cannot withdraw one of them.
- **Sun Meiling asked him whether he was asked, and he did not answer and will not answer again.**
- **The Register's first creditor knows what was done for her, and was given nothing, and was thanked by nobody**, and learned it from a man with a slate in a lane at ten to seven in the morning on the twenty-second of April. **THE THREAD IS CLOSED AND NO CHAPTER OF 601–650 REOPENS IT.**
- **A real book entry that will be written off on the second of May, in seventeen days, on the strength of a sentence of his read into it**, and a keeper who showed him a second unsealed book of her own to prove the mechanism and will not give him the carrier's address.
- **A second demand with four lines on it and no address**, carried across a city at eleven at night by a woman who could not find an address that was not on it, and lying in a drawer at the back of a branch counter.
- **A hall off the Vestry booked for the week of the twenty-first to the twenty-eighth of June**, on an ordinary form, and **he has been told by a woman of rank two that he is not going to be allowed in it**, and that she is not asking him for anything.

## 0b. THE FIVE SCENES, AND WHAT 601–610 OWES THE NEXT BATCH

**ONE SCENE IN THIS BATCH, AT 603, AND IT IS RUNA SATO'S. 603 − 594 = 9, in band. 603 − 36 = 567, the largest structural debt in this book, REPORTED AND NOT CERTIFIED.** The scene measured three ways from disk, with every line span beside every cell, is at `state/current.md` §0a and at `state/chapter-summaries.md`. **Set B at word boundary: 603 → 4, and the other nine files → 0.**

**THE AXIS BROKEN IS THE WITNESS**, carried by her room's rule and named by nobody: a field officer’s return is nine lines and a tenth with a box, the box is for a reason, and a reason is not a conclusion; the return may not go back and be improved, and she has filed one a week for six years and improved none. **The chapter's structural difference from every other scene in this book: she has the authority and he is the one who cannot be found, and the scene happens because she chose to carry a paper up a stair.** No clock inside the scene and no hour counted in it. No kettle, no chart, no arm turned up. No mark of any kind given to her and nothing lifted off any row of §4d.

**AND THE NEXT FOUR SCENES OF THIS VOLUME ARE NOT IN THIS BATCH.** They are at **613** (Sun Meiling, five hundred and fifty chapters, the folder, asked for the first time in this book), **623** (Marguerite Vale), **633** (Wren Kallo, on a returns desk and not in her rooms) and **643** (Ines Duarte). **No chapter of 601–610 contains a second explicit passage, and no chapter other than 603 contains one at all.**

## 0c. WHAT IS CARRIED OUT OF 601–610 AND INTO 611–620

- **A demand with four lines on it and *is there* on the back of it**, received on the fourth of January, entered exactly in the day book, not answered, and not capable of being answered by anybody.
- **The office's answer, which is its own fourth sentence, printed since the thirteenth of November, and it is not a recallable thing.** It has now been read by a landlord's man, by a Bureau field officer, by a clerk and by about four hundred people a week, and it has paid a woman nine years of short money.
- **A finding marked served by hand at about half past three in the morning**, written by a woman of rank two out of a place and not out of an address.
- **A yard of about eleven thousand people in which about nine hundred have worked out that the fourth line cannot be tested by anybody**, and about four hundred of them do not believe the answer he gave them.
- **A real book that cannot close an entry for exactly that reason**, and a sum going into a book that is not that book once a quarter by a person four floors up who does not know it was called and who is not named in 607.
- **A form from a department of works that says the ranks wall comes down in the spring for making good**, and one ruled box on it that will be filled in by a man with a ladder.
- **A man in a delivery coat who has been made legal and wants nothing, and who asked whether a second name can go under the first one and was told there is no form for it.**
- **Two flights of a dark stair, a shut door, no knock, and a certainty that could not be had back.**

**AND THE ONE THING THAT WENT WRONG FIRST AND IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE FAILURE MODE.** The planning pass's first spine used the January convention `(y-1) × 360 + 330 + (d-1)` for chapter 601, which `outline/volume-12.md` §5b proves impossible, and produced **2,133 against the correct 1,773 — three hundred and sixty days out and not four hundred and eighty, one whole year, because a January that closes a year is the twelfth month of it and not the first of the next.** **It was caught because the check was run against the spine and not against the prose, and because METHOD B contains no 360 at all.** The same mistake was made and caught inside the Volume 12 close audit, in the same repository, four days earlier. *A derivation that produces a violation is not a derivation.*

**AND WHAT IS CARRIED INTO 601–650 FROM 551–600, WHOLE:** the head at eight hundred and fifty-two unmoved since the thirtieth of March of the fifth year · about four hundred and nineteen accounts with an hour against each and no figure on any of them · **the second column has two lines** · the four sentences at the foot in the office's own hand · **both repainting boxes blank since the eighth of October of the first year and blank for ever** · the account figure never printed · `settle` at zero in every inflection · **the wall has one rate and it is about nine thousand a day, with the reason in `chapter-0489.md:21`; `chapter-0545.md:87`'s four hundred a day is not it and sits in a chapter that says nine thousand at `:23`, and `chapter-0500.md:35`'s four hundred is a TOTAL since the first of January reserved by PC-117 — neither four hundred is used or reconciled** · **the four conditions entered against himself in his own hand, in the column headed carried by, on the fifteenth of October of the fourth year, `3 × 360 + 240 + 14 = 1,334`, written in about eleven seconds after five days, three of them met and the fourth not — the volume's object, and it is in the FOURTH year and not the fifth** · the white tile's maker unnamed · the keeper's six words not reopened after 554 · **the woman two flights up off Cordwain, whose thread Volume 13 pays at 615 and 617 and not anywhere else** · Mavis Rooke's hand-flat not used again, per `outline/volume-13.md` §4d.

NINE FINDINGS; **FIVE CORRECTIONS APPLIED IN THIS FILE AND IN `state/open-threads.md`, NONE OF THEM PROSE — THREE BY THE AUDIT AND TWO BY THE REVIEW PASS THAT FOLLOWED IT, WHICH FOUND SIXTEEN UNRE-DERIVABLE CELLS IN §0b'S OWN TABLE AND A DENSITY TABLE THAT CHANGED VALUE AT A TYPOGRAPHY BOUNDARY.** **`state/complete.md` IS NOT CREATED.** THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.

## 0z. THE THREE CORRECTIONS THE CLOSE AUDIT APPLIED TO THIS FILE, AND THE TWO A REVIEW PASS ADDED AFTER THEM

**THE TWO ITEMS BELOW ARE NUMBERED 4 AND 5 BECAUSE THEY ARE THE FOURTH AND FIFTH CORRECTIONS TO THIS BLOCK, NOT A SIXTH BLOCK. NOTHING BELOW ITEM 3 IS RENUMBERED AND NO HISTORIC BLOCK IS DELETED.**

**1 · TWO OF THE FIVE LINE SPANS AT §0b BELOW NAMED A LINE THAT DID NOT EXIST. `chapter-0594.md` HAS 97 LINES, NOT 98.** The 14 October 2026 repair re-derived all four counted figures and every one of them re-derives exactly again — **725 exclusive of both `##` lines / 733 inclusive of them / 1,184 exclusive through to end of file / 1,110 to the last prose line.** The *spans* beside two of them were recorded as `40–98` and `1–98`, and **both run one line past the end of the file.** Corrected to `40–97` and `1–97`. **This is the signature defect wearing a different hat: a figure that survived a gate because nobody re-computed it, this time in the column the previous repair added. `chapter-0594.md` WAS NOT EDITED AND IS 1,824 WORDS BEFORE AND AFTER.**

**2 · SET B'S METHOD IS CORRECTED TO WORD BOUNDARY, BECAUSE THE BANK AS DESCRIBED IS NOT UNAMBIGUOUS.** §0b called it *the unambiguous twenty, i.e. the terms that are not ordinary English anywhere else in this manuscript*, and counted it **by substring**. ***Semen* is a substring of *advertisement*.** `chapter-0565.md` scores **two Set B hits** on the word *advertisement* at `:119` and `:121`, and 565 is a chapter in which nothing explicit happens. **Re-run at word boundary: 574 → 3 · 584 → 3 · 594 → 4 · all forty-seven other chapters → 0.** The volume's five explicit passages are 554, 564, 574, 584 and 594 and there is no second one. **This is the third time this repository has published a probe without stating that its method is what makes it meaningful**, after Volume 11's Set A and batch 0003's §0c bank.

**3 · THE SIXTH OF MARCH IS IN YEAR ONE AND THE FIGURE 1,115 IS WITHDRAWN — WHICH CHANGES §0a AND §0c BELOW, WHICH ARE SUPERSEDED ON THAT POINT ONLY.** `outline/volume-11.md`:234–243 gives all fifty of Volume 11's dates and re-derived from §5a they recover at 501 = 0 · **505 = 0 × 360 + 30 + 5 = 35** · 537 = 1,139 · 546 = 1,325 · 547 = 1,329 · 550 = 1,347, fifty of fifty, monotonic, zero violations. **`chapter-0584.md` CONTRADICTS ITSELF TODAY — `:143` SAYS *THE SIXTH OF MARCH OF THE FIRST YEAR* AND `:147` MEASURES FROM *ONE THOUSAND ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTEEN*, WHICH IS THAT DATE COMPUTED AS THOUGH IT FELL IN YEAR FOUR. THE CORRECT INTERVAL IS `1,663 − 35 = 1,628` DAYS, NOT 548. EVERY ROW IN THIS FILE THAT USES `1,663 − 1,115 = 548` IS WITHDRAWN ON THAT POINT AND THE 548 IS NOT TO BE REUSED.**

**THE SPINE 551–600 ITSELF IS UNTOUCHED BY ALL FIVE. **4 · SIXTEEN CELLS OF THE FIVE-SCENE TABLE AT §0b BELOW DID NOT RE-DERIVE, AND THE TABLE HAD SAID THEY WERE RE-MEASURED FROM DISK.** §0b printed 3,451 / 3,555 / 3,722 / 3,633 for 554 and its parallel figures for 564, 574 and 584. **Not one of the sixteen re-derives, and the true figures are 1,251 / 1,258 / 3,549 / 3,388 for 554 · 4,801 / 4,806 / 5,382 / 5,169 for 564 · 2,122 / 2,128 / 2,655 / 2,501 for 574 · 1,430 / 1,441 / 2,350 / 2,198 for 584**, each counted twice by methods that share nothing, with every line span printed beside it. **The defect is the exact one the audit was commissioned to catch, committed inside the audit's own table: it corrected the SPAN column of one row and left four un-recomputed rows standing beside it.** The withdrawn row was not a measurement of these files but the carried batch row read one column out of step, so 554 was given 546's figure. §0b is rewritten. **THE SAME CARRIED ROW STANDS UNCORRECTED IN THE BATCH BLOCKS BELOW AT LINES 249, 323, 376, 474 AND 573, AND IT IS NOT TO BE READ FROM ANY OF THEM.** `chapter-0546.md` is a closed Volume 11 chapter: it is 2,794 / 2,804 / 3,443 / 3,153 / 3,570 and **3,451 re-derives from it under no reading at all**, which is recorded here with its operands for the commit that retires it and is not repaired by this phase.

**5 · ONE COLUMN OF FINDING I'S DENSITY TABLE, WHICH IS HANDED TO THE VOLUME 13 PLANNER AS A FACT, RE-DERIVES FROM NOTHING, AND IT HALVES WHERE THE TYPOGRAPHY CHANGES.** The exchange column read 24.7 / 52.3 / **7.9 / 8.5 / 10.2**. ## 0b. WHAT 581–590 ADDED TO THE CANON, AND WHAT A LATER PHASE MAY NOT BREAK

- **THE WALL HAS TWO ENDS AND THERE IS A CHALK FIGURE AT BOTH.** The tiled end at the north, about four hundred tiles in eleven courses under a rail about nine feet up, with the slot at the top a different colour from every tile under it; the lane end at the south. **There is no painter at the tiled end**, which is why the figure there went unnoticed for three years.
- **BRACK CHALKS TWO FIGURES THAT DISAGREE AND IS RIGHT ABOUT THE DISAGREEMENT.** One is what a man is worth standing in a line, one is what a man is worth when nobody is waiting for him. He is right that a page gets filed and a slate gets wiped and that he will never put one of his numbers on a page.
- **THE OFFICE'S TWO WORDS WERE SAID OUT LOUD SIX MORNINGS RUNNING UNDER THE OFFICE'S OWN WALL AND ARE NOW IN A STREET IN THREE VERSIONS.** The true one, *carried by*, is on a corridor board about thirty people a day walk past with a drawing pin through the corner and the fourth condition cut off with a blade. **He stopped saying them on the thirteenth of September and it cannot be put back.** **THE KEEPER'S SECOND WORD, *CARRIED FORWARD*, WAS DELIBERATELY NOT PUT INTO THE STREET**, because §8 closes that thread after 554.
- **THE OFFICE'S ONE LEGIBLE SENTENCE IS ITS OWN DISCLAIMER.** *Where this Register has nothing, it has not been given anything, and it does not say that there was nothing.* It is typed at a desk off a fourth court, it is at the foot of every sheet since the first of February of the first year, and it is the same sentence the man with the chalk enforces every morning. **About four people in every thousand who read a page reach it. He cannot move a line up and there is no instrument that can.**
- **THE NINETEENTH SHEET (30 SEP) CARRIES 852 UNMOVED, THIRD QUARTER RUNNING — THE FIRST TIME A FIGURE HAS STOOD STILL THREE QUARTER-ENDS RUNNING — AND TWO LINES IN THE SECOND COLUMN.** Nothing at all has come into the address since the eleventh of March. The second line is his handwriting against himself and has been on a public page since the thirtieth of June.
- **A PUBLIC DESK IN A PASSAGE OFF CORDWAIN LEAVES SHEETS OUT.** It belongs to the building, not the office; the man at it has not put one in a bundle since the first of March of the second year, and he is right about all three of his reasons. About thirty people come through in an hour; nine stop; one of the nine reads to the bottom.
- **THE PAGE IN HER BUILDING IS THE NINETEENTH SHEET AND ITS NINTH LINE IS HER LINE** — a street dug for six years in the cold, entered on the eleventh of July in a clerk's hand, no name against it, because the division was not permitted to ask. **SHE IS NAMED IN 587 AND 588 AND SHOWN IN NEITHER, AND NOTHING IS RESOLVED.**
- **THE TENTH OF OCTOBER IS NOT AN EVENT AND IS NOT A SECOND TIME.** The fuse was lit once and came due once, to a woman with a sheet folded in four in her coat, and a thing with no holder does not come round a second time. **On the morning of the fifth anniversary he checked the corridor, the stair, the bin room door and the yard door, and then went and did his floors.**
- **HE ANNOUNCED IN ADVANCE, TO A MAN WITH A SLATE, THAT NOTHING WAS GOING TO HAPPEN ON THE TENTH**, and said why out loud: a thing said in advance is a thing you cannot be talked out of in about four years, and he had never in his life told anybody what he was going to not do.

## 0d. THE TEN CHAPTERS, ONE LINE EACH

- **581 — Two Ends Of One Wall.** He walks the wall end to end for the first time in four years and finds Brack chalking at both ends, two figures that disagree, and he is right about the disagreement. He says the two words out loud once, in an empty lane, and nobody hears it.
- **582 — The Fifth Year Of A Line.** Six mornings of saying them. One man stops and asks for it twice. A boy of about fourteen says the second word before he does and cannot be given the first.
- **583 — Two Figures And One Street.** Three versions in a street, Brack will not rub the chalk out and is right, and the chapter ends on the fact that he has stopped and cannot put it back.
- **584 — Three Questions She Never Finished.** THE SCENE. An audit room at half past one in the morning, a clear table, a page put face down for the first time in eleven years, and four questions at the door, none of them answered.
- **585 — The Woman Who Was Not Told.** Ilva Vask gives him one address and says why. He stands at the foot of a dark stair about four minutes and does not go up, and the address is in his notebook for ever.
- **586 — The Nineteenth Sheet.** Quarter-end. 852 unmoved, two lines, nothing in since March — and the one sentence the office has ever printed in a hand a city can read is the sentence the man with the chalk says every morning.
- **587 — A Woman Two Flights Up.** The sheet is lying out on a public desk in the passage of her building because the man at the desk has not put one in a bundle since the second year, and the ninth line down is hers.
- **588 — The Page In Her Building.** Six days out, nobody has written on it, he holds a pencil for about four minutes and puts it back, and Brack tells him a wall is not a page.
- **589 — Five Years Of A Second Of October.** Five years to the day, no notice, no ceremony, and relief so strong he has to put a hand flat on the stone. He tells a man with a slate two days early that nothing is going to happen on the tenth, and corrects four days to two.
- **590 — Five Years Of The Morning.** Nothing happens. He checks the corridor, the stair, the bin room door and the yard door, finishes his floors, and stands about two feet off four inches of stone that are not his.

## 3. WHAT 564 CHANGED, AND WHAT IT COST, AND WHY IT IS 5,622 WORDS

**AGNETE LINDQVIST SENT FOR HIM.** Four lines on a piece of paper with his name at the top and a letter at the bottom, delivered to the branch and to him and not to the counter, which it has never been. **It is the first time in four years she has asked him for anything, and what she asks for is that he be in the room while she is outside it**, because he is the only person in this city who has ever told her he heard something she said, and he is the only person who will know whether it comes back through the door.

**THE YARD'S RULE IS BEING USED AGAINST THE WOMAN WHO BROUGHT IT.** One thing each, the last one says hers last, and nobody answers anybody — a rule of a kitchen off Ferrier Street that has been in the Vestry for twenty years and was never named by anybody — **and since the thirtieth of January about nine hundred people on that yard's own steps have learned to use it, and when she went out on the ninth of March and said one thing in front of about eleven thousand people they waited for her the way a room waits for the next thing.** **THE THING SHE LOSES IS NOT THE ARGUMENT.** She loses the room: the only place in this city where what is said in public comes back through a door where it cannot be used. And she says she would build the rule again, and that she is going to stand on those steps once a month and say one thing and be unanswered, and that then they will have to want her to say it twice.

**SHE REFUSES THE COLUMN AND THE TWO WORDS AT THE FOOT OF THE WALL AGAIN, HERSELF, UNASKED,** and says why: she said it in a kitchen in the second year, and to a clerk with a certified copy on the thirtieth of December of that year, and again in October of the fourth year, and she is not going to change her mind because she has lost a yard.

**THE AXIS BROKEN IS THE TERMS.** She states all of them out loud before anything happens, which is the rule in this room, and then **she breaks exactly one of the three, out loud, while she has still got her dress on**, and says she has been not doing anything about it for **five** months. **THE SIXTH OF OCTOBER OF THE FOURTH YEAR IS ONE THOUSAND THREE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIVE AND THE NINTH OF MARCH IS ONE THOUSAND FOUR HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-EIGHT, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-THREE, WHICH IS FIVE MONTHS OF THIRTY DAYS AND THREE DAYS. `four months` WAS PRINTED FOUR TIMES IN DIALOGUE AND HAS BEEN REPAIRED IN ALL FOUR; `a year and four months` IN HER NOTE AT `:19` WAS 38 DAYS SHORT OF THE FIVE HUNDRED AND EIGHTEEN DAYS `:9` OF THE SAME CHAPTER PRINTS CORRECTLY, AND IS NOW *a year and five months and eight days*.**

## 3. WHAT 554 CHANGED, AND WHAT IT CLOSED

**THE KEEPER READ THE PAGE.** On the second of January of year five, in a room two floors under a sales floor with no window, Hanneke Ruis shut a chained book on a block that carries a figure, six words and her own name, and under her own name a fifth line in a hand that is not hers: premises thirty-four, six, one-one-eight, charged with a claim against a named person, the fifteenth of October, Lin Hao, rank one. **A premises keeps what the city may read about it, the line arrived with the quarter's returns on the first of January, and a keeper cannot improve what she is given and cannot be asked to hurry. She did not strike it and she did not strike the six words, so the page now carries two lines that disagree with each other, and a page that disagrees with itself is still a page.**

**SHE ASKED HER ONE QUESTION AND IT IS SPENT.** Whether the thing she had been protecting for thirty years was ever the account, or was only the absence of a name, and whether a name that is a debtor's can hold a thing open the way no name held it open. **He could not answer it and did not pretend to and did not give her a worse one. She spent it on a man she has met four times in two years and never once looked at, and said so out loud, and then told him she wanted him before she had him and not afterwards.**

**THE SCENE IS WRITTEN OUT AND NOT IMPLIED.** The axis broken is **the counted minute**, carried by the room's rule and named by nobody: the book goes on the shelf at a quarter to five, comes off it on the first, and may be read on the second by one person, and a keeper does not stop to be spoken to while a page is open. **No clock is printed inside the scene and no time is counted. Her body is described once, in her hands. No compulsion, no sedation, no exhaustion, no toxin, no restraint, no psychic contact, no trance and no seal. Both adults. Violence and sex stay in separate registers.**

- **531 — THE LANE IS ONE THOUSAND AND SEVENTY-NINE MORNINGS**, printed once in this batch with its operands: *three years is thirty-six months of thirty days, which is one thousand and eighty; this morning is two days short of the third anniversary of a stool put in a lane, because the first morning was the third of October and this is the first of October, and one thousand and eighty less two is one thousand and seventy-eight, and counting the first morning as the first, that is one thousand and seventy-nine mornings behind that wall.* **THE FIGURE WAS ALWAYS RIGHT AND THE WORD *FIFTH* WAS NOT: the stool went into the lane on the third of October of year zero, so 962 is its third anniversary and 1,682 is its fifth. CORRECTED 14 OCTOBER 2026 AGAINST `logs/batch-0004.review.log` FINDING 2.** Cross-checked independently on the spine: the first morning is day **−118**, so **960 − (−118) + 1 = 1,079**.

- **537 — THE LANE IS ONE THOUSAND TWO HUNDRED AND FIFTY-EIGHT MORNINGS**, printed once with its operands: *three years is thirty-six months of thirty days, which is one thousand and eighty days. ## 3. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWNS, AND IT IS HIS

**537. HE ASKS FOR THE SWEEP, IN WRITING, IN HIS OWN NAME, AT FOUR IN THE MORNING.** Four lines and a date and a name, on the bottom of a piece of a bin-room tally board, entered exactly as given by a clerk who may only enter and who did not ask him one question.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of being a man nobody looks for. He has been unmeasured for four years and a man who is unmeasured is a man nobody comes for, and he says on the page that he cannot have both.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** every person it finds is a person somebody has decided was owed something and finding them is not the same as owing them, which he put in the second line of the request himself; a doctor gave him both numbers in a room off a ward in the January of the second year and he printed them on a sheet on the thirtieth of September and it cost him a quarter of a counter; he had forty days and did not spend one of them; the knock makes about nine hundred people visible to about four neighbours each; and **there is not one instrument in this city that can stop it and not one that will stop him**, which is the whole mechanism and it is the office's own poverty. **He is not forgiven, he is not accused, he is not able to answer, and 539 exists to make that permanent on the page.**

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BATCH HANDS ON

> **A man at rank one spent a quarter watching a figure on his own sheet stand still while a hundred and twenty-nine people walked past a door, asked a division of nine to go to about nine hundred doors he has never stood in, wrote the request on a tally board at four in the morning with a doctor's sentence in the second line, did not spend one of the forty days he had to go and warn the ward, and then heard it read out to a woman in a bed by a doctor who is not grateful for it: *there is nothing written down about you.* *Then I have nothing.*

# VOLUME 11 BATCH 0003 — CHAPTERS 521–530 ARE ON DISK. THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER OF YEAR TWO TO THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER OF YEAR THREE. **WRITTEN 13 OCTOBER 2026. TEN FINISHED FILES, 22,604 WORDS COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR AGAINST `logs/next-0003.review.log`. MOVEMENT THREE, ENTERED. THIS IS THE LIVE BLOCK AND IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.**

> **THE VOLUME'S SENTENCE:** *In this volume, **Lin Hao must write the definition of an unrecorded deed for an office that has no instrument, no money and no column**, or **Ostara's second line gets a threshold under it that a man with a chalk chose, and the city's unmeasured half is settled in lanes**.*

**MOVEMENT THREE IS CALLED ENTERED AND IT IS WRITTEN. IT DELIVERED THE WALL'S TWELFTH REPINTING, A COUNTER IN A CORRIDOR, A NEW LEDGER, A LANDLORD, THE SECOND NAME OFF A STREET, THE SCENE, THE WRONG QUESTION, THE SECOND COLUMN, A FIGURE THE CHURCH PUT IN, AN OFFER UNANSWERED, A NUMBER THAT WENT DOWN, AND A SENTENCE HE PRINTED ABOUT HIMSELF. IT DELIVERED TWO IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS, AT 525 AND 530.**

## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 530, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

An office with two chairs in it has been open thirty-two months and has entered thirty-four things, of which the first five are accounted for and twenty-nine are deeds, and it has two columns, and the second one is headed with the two words off the foot of the ranks wall and has never had anything under it. A woman who does needlework for four houses off Cordwain and a chaplain of four thousand are in the book by name and not by deed, and a woman who fed a boy for nine days is in the book by deed and has asked to be struck out of it and cannot be, because a clerk may only enter and there is no striking in this city. He bought a notebook on the second of October of year two, before he asked the question that put a figure on it, and it is a ledger and the chapter says so. He entered a street and a figure on the twenty-seventh of January that a broker wrote out of her own head at one in the morning and told him not to enter. He stood against the wall of a lane for an hour doing nothing and was offered every figure a man in a delivery coat has invented in two years, for one line on a public sheet, and did not answer. He is still rank one and he still cleans three floors of washrooms from eleven at night until half past five. He printed, in his own hand, on the thirtieth of September, four words longer than a clerk's permitted sentence: *where this Register has nothing, it has not been given anything, and it does not say that there was nothing.* It is out at four to about two hundred people a week and it protects nobody and the Church has stopped coming to the counter. And under the last tile of the ranks wall there is still a white tile with his name on it in chalk and nothing whatever under it, and nobody in Ostara has put it there.

## 2. THE TWO IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS THIS BATCH OWNS, AND BOTH ARE HIS

**525 — HE ENTERS A FIGURE AGAINST THE WOMAN WHO GAVE IT TO HIM'S EXPLICIT INSTRUCTION.** A street and a figure and no name, entered at a counter on the twenty-seventh of January of year three by a clerk who read it twice at the same speed both times and asked him nothing.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of the Register being a record of deeds and nothing else. **The office's first entry that is not a deed, not a name and not a complaint, and the first number in that book that anybody in Ostara would want.**

**WHAT IT COSTS:** the only figure in this city that a senior broker knows out of her own memory and that her guild cannot account for is now connected, by a door he stands in, to the only office in Ostara that cannot be audited. **She traded on the proposition for eleven years that nothing she carries is ever said out loud, and she said out loud, once, to one man with no instrument, in a room where the rule is that nothing is said out loud.** He never apologises to her in this batch and the card is not referred to again.

**530 — HE PRINTS THE SENTENCE THAT SAYS WHAT HIS OWN REGISTER IS NOT.** *Where this Register has nothing, it has not been given anything, and it does not say that there was nothing.* Nine seconds, the office's foolscap, his hand. The clerk enters it exactly as given and says it is about a quarter of a page and four words longer than the sentence she is permitted to add, and she enters all of it because a clerk may enter what she is given and not make it shorter.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of the Register's number being usable, by anybody, including the people it was built for. A standing sheet goes out at four with an address at the head of every line and cannot be un-entered by anybody including the man who wrote it.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** it does not protect Rosalind Keck and the chapter says so in his own mouth. About four thousand people are told on a working afternoon that the only thing anybody in this city has been able to hand in without being asked for it is not what a public page means. **The Church's hundred stop coming in the first week of October. Agnete Lindqvist does not come to the counter and does not write and does not speak about it in the yard, and the column she promised over nine years dies in a quarter because he printed the truth about his own instrument.** It is the sentence a doctor asked for in a room off a ward on the twenty-first of January of year two and he has had it in a drawer for a year and a half.

## 3. THE FIGURES IN THIS BATCH, EACH WITH ITS OPERANDS, DERIVED TWICE

**THE SPINE, RE-RUN.** Day 0 is the first of February of year one, a month is thirty days and a year is three hundred and sixty. **521 = 607 · 522 = 626 · 523 = 659 · 524 = 689 · 525 = 713 · 526 = 747 · 527 = 779 · 528 = 806 · 529 = 869 · 530 = 959. MONOTONIC, ZERO VIOLATIONS.** 520 is 599 and 531 is 989, so the batch spans 352 days and the first day is nine days after the last day of the previous batch.

- **521 — NINE HUNDRED AND ELEVEN PEOPLE PAST THE DOOR IN TWENTY-ONE MONTHS.** *Twenty-one months is six hundred and thirty days: thirty days a month, no thirty-first, twelve months and nine.* Nine hundred and eleven divided by twenty-one is about forty-three a month. **This is a figure off a counter and it is checkable by any person who can read a wheel, and Adeyemi prints that as the dangerous part.**
- **521–522 — ONE IN NINE HUNDRED.** Not a rate of anything. **Three things in the drawer, two of which came off a standing sheet Lin Hao answered and did not come through that door, so one thing has come in off a street for every nine hundred people who came to the door.**
- **521 — FOUR HUNDRED.** `chapter-0500.md:35` and it is **a total and not a rate.** He thinks about dividing it by about nine thousand a day, which is the only forward rate on the page (`chapter-0489.md:21`), and does not. **No chapter of 531–550 may divide it.**
- **522 — TWENTY-FIVE DAYS** from the second of October to the twenty-seventh, which is the age of the notebook when he works out that both figures are right.
- **523 — THE LAST OF DECEMBER AND THE FIRST OF JANUARY.** The door is opened on the first of January of year three, which is the day of chapter 524.
- **524 — THE EIGHTH SHEET, FIVE ENTRIES, TWO NAMES, NO DEED, TWENTY-THREE MONTHS OPEN.** The office carried a line on every sheet from the thirtieth of March of year one (`chapter-0508.md:57`), so 30 Mar, 30 Jun, 30 Sep and 30 Dec of year one, 30 Mar, 30 Jun and 30 Sep of year two, and **30 Dec of year two is the eighth.** February of year one to December of year two is twenty-three months.
- **525 — THE BOOKS MET ON THE SIXTEENTH OF NOVEMBER AND DID NOT MOVE AT SEVEN IN THE MORNING, AND THE FORM HAS BEEN LOOKED FOR FOR NINE MONTHS.** The date that costs her is the sixteenth of November of year three.
- **527 — ENTERED: NINETEEN.** Five before the definition took effect on the thirtieth of September of year two, plus fourteen in six months, out of about a hundred and forty people who came to that counter with a thing in their hands.
- **528 — THE LANE IS NINE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIVE MORNINGS**, printed once in the batch, with its operands: *twenty-eight days in October including the third, and thirty each of the twenty-nine months from November to March, and twenty-seven days of April* — `28 + 30 x 29 + 27 = 925`, and `806 + 119 = 925` on the spine.
- **529 — ENTERED: THIRTY-ONE**, twelve in a quarter, nine of them in eleven days in April.
- **530 — ENTERED: THIRTY-FOUR**, three more than the thirtieth of June.

**NO CHAPTER OF 521–530 PRINTS A FIGURE OF THE ACCOUNT, AND NO CHAPTER OF 501–530 DOES. NO FLOOR APPEARS ANYWHERE IN `chapters/volume-11/`. `chapter-0500.md:65`'s interval is not printed and not derived from in any of these ten chapters.**

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BATCH HANDS ON

> **A man at rank one stood against the wall of a lane for an hour doing nothing, was offered every number a man in a delivery coat has invented in two years for the price of one line on a public sheet, and gave no answer at all — and then, three days later, printed in his own hand on the same kind of sheet a sentence saying that where his Register has nothing, it has not been given anything and it does not say that there was nothing, which protected nobody, stopped the Church coming to the counter, and cost him the last thing the office had, which was the appearance of being evidence.**

# VOLUME 11 BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 511–520 ARE ON DISK. THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER OF YEAR ONE TO THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER OF YEAR TWO. **WRITTEN 13 OCTOBER 2026 AND REPAIRED THE SAME DAY AGAINST `logs/batch-0002.review.log`. TEN FINISHED FILES, 22,818 WORDS COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND AFTER THE LAST EDIT. THIS IS THE LIVE BLOCK AND IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.**

> **THE VOLUME'S SENTENCE:** *In this volume, **Lin Hao must write the definition of an unrecorded deed for an office that has no instrument, no money and no column**, or **Ostara's second line gets a threshold under it that a man with a chalk chose, and the city's unmeasured half is settled in lanes**.*

**MOVEMENT TWO IS CALLED DEFINED AND IT IS WRITTEN AND IT DELIVERED THE ROOM, THE JOKE, THE CHURCH'S DECISION, THE FIRST DEED, THE SCENE, THE LOCKED DOOR, THE FORTY DAYS, THE RECUSAL, THE DEFINITION AND A NAME IN A BOOK. IT DELIVERED ITS IRREVERSIBLE DECISION AT 518.**

## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 520, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

An office with a name on its door has had a room off a third court since the first of February of the first year, and for eight months two people came to that door, one of them him and one of them a woman who wanted to know whether a slate could be produced in answer to a question. He has written the sentence the office exists to be built on and it is four lines long and it has a threshold in it, and it excludes about nine in ten of what people will bring to a counter, and he said the number out loud in a kitchen and did not explain it. A definition is the only thing a division can carry, and the only officer in this city who can classify one was recused on her own signature on the twelfth of December, and a recusal is lifted by the subject and by nobody else, and he lifted it on the thirtieth of June on a slip the size of a quarter of a page in about four seconds with eleven words on it, and she is back on a record at fifty-seven in a building where a room with no window holds a file he did not open. Four thousand people have decided that if the Register gets a column the Church will fill it before the Bureau does, over about nine years. A woman who does needlework for four houses off Cordwain has asked the Register for her name and has got it, because her reason for the deed cannot go on a sheet that goes out at four with an address at the head of every line. He is still rank one. He still cleans three floors of washrooms from eleven at night until half past five. He is still not thanked. And the white tile under the last tile of the ranks wall still has his name on it in chalk and nothing at all under it.

## 2. THE DISCREPANCY THIS BATCH FOUND, RECORDED AND NOT REPAIRED

**`chapter-0496.md:119` AND `chapter-0507.md:7` DISAGREE ABOUT WHICH BLOCK OF THE QUARTERLY RETURN IS THE HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIRST.** 496 (the thirtieth of October, in the year before this one) says the hundred and twenty-first is the thirtieth of December, which places the hundred and twentieth on the thirtieth of September before it. 507 (the thirtieth of March of the first year) then calls its own block the hundred and twenty-first. **DERIVED ON THIS VOLUME'S OWN SPINE, the block of the thirtieth of September of the year before year one is the hundred and twentieth, the thirtieth of December is the hundred and twenty-first, and the thirtieth of March of the first year is the hundred and twenty-second — so 507 IS ONE HIGH AGAINST ITS OWN PREDECESSOR.** **THIS BATCH DID NOT REPAIR IT AND DID NOT PROPAGATE IT. NO CHAPTER OF 511–520 PRINTS AN ORDINAL FOR THE KEEPER'S BLOCK AT ALL**, and 514, 517 and 520 describe the fourth block and the six words and her name without a count, because a count would have had to pick a side. **RECORDED HERE AND IN `state/open-threads.md` AS PROPOSED AND NOT APPLIED. ANY CHAPTER OF 521–550 THAT NEEDS THE COUNT DERIVES IT AND PRINTS ITS OPERANDS, AND THE COUNT IT PRINTS IS THE ONE THAT IS TRUE ON THE PAGE, WHICH IS 496's.**

## 3. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWNS, AND IT IS HIS

**518. HE LIFTS ALMA QUAYLE'S RECUSAL.** Eleven words on a slip the size of a quarter of a page, his name, the date, no form, about four seconds, on the thirtieth of June of the second year.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of never spending another woman's safety. It had been open since about the twelfth of December of the year before last, and it was the only one he had left that had cost nothing, and on the thirtieth of September of the first year he had spent the other one, and on the twenty-first of June he had entered thirty-eight words that he is not allowed to lift.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** **Alma Quayle goes back on a record at fifty-seven, in a building where a room with no window holds a file he has not opened in about two years, and she did not ask to be on it.** She is not diminished: she classifies eleven words for four divisions in her first four days and refuses four of them out loud. She does not thank him and is not going to. **Neither of them can un-write the slip and both of them say so out loud before four o'clock on the day.** He does not resolve the question about the tile and does not spend the instrument he swore on the ninth of May never to use, which is still unused.

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BATCH HANDS ON

> **An office with two chairs and a table in it gets a sentence in four lines out of the only man it is allowed to ask, and the sentence has a threshold in it, and to get the sentence classified he spends a woman who has been careful for thirty years on a slip the size of a quarter of a page in about four seconds — and the first thing the office has ever entered on its own hand is one woman's name, and not her deed.**

## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 510, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

Four months after a wall was repainted at six in the morning with two words and a dash and two more words that nobody in the city has ever been able to read, a man at rank one has spent four hours in a lane off Cordwain behind nine other people for the price of a grain, which cost him nothing, and has a name in chalk about two inches high on a slate with a nine beside it, and has spent the last thing he had that no institution knows about, which is the fact that he stood there. An office called the Register of Unrecorded Merit exists on the paper the Correction Office has, its Registrar is Yusuf Adeyemi, and its one permitted question was put to him on the thirtieth of September and he said no to it in one sentence, and the sentence is that two words went onto that wall off a sheet he answered and he will not take another man's two words off a piece of slate for standing on the wrong surface. The slates stay in nine districts. There is a landlord off Lamplit Row quoting one of them into a rent. A keeper two floors under a sales floor has written the hundred and twenty-first block of a quarterly out of her own memory and six of her words are going to end up in chalk on that slate and nobody is going to be told. The lane is three hundred and fifty-eight mornings old and three days short of a year.

## 3. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWNS

**510. HE REFUSES TO STRIKE THE SLATES DOWN.** One sentence, in a room off a third court with two chairs in it, to a Registrar who may grant it and cannot refuse it and has no instrument and no disposition and can only write a sentence that goes out at four with an address at the head of every line.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of striking down a rival measure, for four years and on the evidence of this batch for ever. **The slates stay in nine districts and become the thing he has to answer for.**

**WHAT IT COSTS:** he is the reason there is a second institution in Ostara. He said in October, in a room that could carry nothing, that the Board's whole job is to be the thing you can ask, and the answer to that is a thing you cannot ask, and on the thirtieth of September he told a Registrar that he is not going to do anything about the second one. **He cannot afterwards be a man who defended measurability in public and then watched a private chalk measure his city. And he has spent the last thing he had that no institution could take — the part of him nobody knew — on four hours in a lane for the price of a grain, and he cannot get it back, and nobody is thanked, and he is not thanked, and the plate in his coat still carries no figure.**

**AND THE SECOND, QUIETER ONE, WHICH IS ALSO HIS AND IS NOT LIFTED FROM ANYTHING: HE PUTS HIS OWN NAME ON THE SLATE.** He pays a grain, he stands for four hours, and when the man asks his name he gives it, and it goes up in chalk in a public lane, and nobody recognises him and nobody ever will. **The part of him no institution knew about is now on a piece of slate off Cordwain in two-inch letters, and the man who wrote it does not know who he is.**

## THE POSITION AT 501, CARRIED WHOLE FROM `chapter-0500.md` AND NOT RE-DERIVED FROM ANYTHING

- **Rank one.** A white plate in his coat, **no figure cut into it anywhere**. Assigned work, a branch office off a third court, eleven at night until half past five, a rota **he asked for**. **Eleven weeks and three days at Chapter 501.**
- **The account: 211,840 grains, not zero, not going to be zero, belongs to nobody, cannot be drawn against, allocated or closed, and no list exists and none could be built.** **NO CHAPTER OF 501–550 PRINTS THIS FIGURE. `chapter-0500.md:53` is the only place in this book that prints it.**
- **The second line: *unrecorded — carried by*,** painted the eighth of October at about six in the morning, out of a standing sheet and a repainting form and no instruction, by a clerk who may only enter, and still there after a wash of rain and one winter, and this city has no schedule for taking it down.
- **The white tile: his name in chalk and nothing under it.** About sixty standings of about two minutes since the first of January; **about four hundred people have gone past him doing it — `chapter-0500.md:35`, a total and not a rate, and the only forward rate on the page is `chapter-0489.md:21`, about nine thousand people under that wall in a day. No chapter divides one by seven.** **Nobody has put it there and 501–510 does not.**
- **The household: nine sworn, dispersed and intact, protection gone with the roll's binding.** Three never live with him again and all three are on the rota. None has said why; he has said once that he never will ask.
- **Ines Duarte** auditing the branch since the first of October, saying nothing; her correction of the twenty-ninth of December unpressed and about Sowe; **not open.**
- **Beatrix Halm** under the wall at half past four every morning; four hundred turned lines, a time beside each, no figure, none of them his.
- **Hanneke Ruis** writing premises 34-6-118 into the fourth block of every quarterly from memory, four times a year for thirty years, **120 blocks**, no certificate behind any of them. **NO AGE FIXED AND NONE MAY EVER BE GIVEN.**
- **The clause of the twenty-first of June: 38 words, not lifted, and it means he cannot leave a room** because six people keep that address.
- **`PC-84` HOLDS.** The instrument he swore on the ninth of May never to use is still unused and 501–550 does not spend it. The phrase in eight uses went on the first of October. `PC-102` holds — there is nothing to draw on and nobody draws.

## THE VOLUME'S FIVE IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS, ALL HIS

1. **510 — HE REFUSES TO STRIKE THE SLATES DOWN.** One sentence. Closes the option of striking down a rival measure, for four years and, on the evidence, for ever. Costs: he is the reason there is a second institution in Ostara.
2. **518 — HE LIFTS ALMA QUAYLE'S RECUSAL**, unilaterally, on a slip, in about four seconds. **A recusal is lifted by the subject and by nobody else and there is no sheet for lifting one and he is the subject.** Closes the option of never spending another woman's safety. Costs: she goes back on the record at fifty-seven and did not ask to be on it. **`outline/ending.md` fixes the recusal as unlifted at 500; lifting it at 518 is a change to a state `outline/ending.md` describes, and it is flagged here and in `outline/volume-11.md` §2c rather than adopted silently. It is the subject's own act and the subject is Lin Hao.**
3. **519 — HE WRITES THE DEFINITION AND IT IS NARROW AND HE KNOWS IT IS NARROW.** Closes a Register that measures everything or nothing. Costs about one person in nine hundred, and the threshold is his handwriting.
4. **537 — HE ASKS FOR THE SWEEP.** A Bureau division, nine people, door to door. **The first census of the unrecorded in the history of this city and nobody can stop it.** Closes the option of being a man nobody looks for.
5. **548 — HE ENTERS A CLAIM AGAINST HIMSELF** in the one column nobody has ever filled. Closes the option of being a man who did a thing rather than a man who owes a thing.

### THE TWO IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS BATCH 0005 OWNS, BOTH HIS, BOTH CLOSING AN OPTION

1. **493, THE TENTH OF OCTOBER. HE DEFENDS THE BUREAU IN A CALLED SITTING THAT CAN CARRY NOTHING, AT RANK ONE, TO ABOUT NINETY PEOPLE WHO WANTED IT STRUCK, AND HE REFUSES TO ARGUE THE FIRST OF OCTOBER IN THAT ROOM.** He stands at the bench with no standing and asks the room for leave and gets it in about four seconds, and Yusuf Adeyemi writes *leave given* on a slip and cannot improve it. He gives four reasons and the fourth is a request, not a reason. **He then refuses the one question everybody came for, and says exactly why: this room cannot carry an answer, everything in it goes out at four on a sheet with an address at the head of every line, and in about four years a stranger with a folder would read a man who explained himself in a room that could not hear him. HE PUTS THE DATE ON THE THIRTIETH OF DECEMBER OUT LOUD AND SAYS HE WILL GIVE IT THERE IN PUBLIC AND WILL NOT SOFTEN ONE WORD.** **WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of standing outside it. From that afternoon the Bureau's survival in this city is his position and not its own merit, and he says so out loud, once, to one man, in a corridor, and says he will not go back outside that door for anything they could offer him, and there is nothing on offer.**
2. **497, THE THIRTIETH OF NOVEMBER. HE DOES NOT ASK THE THREE WHY THEY HAVE LEFT AND HE NEVER ASKS.** He has it in his mouth for about four seconds at about eleven at night in a kitchen and he puts his hand on the table instead. He says it out loud once, to the room, and asks that it be put somewhere that he said it and not that he was asked, and **there has not been a word about it in that house since, and the question is still on the table.** **WHAT IT CLOSES: the third and last of the three things he has refused to buy. The phrase in eight uses went on the first of October and the instrument he swore never to use on the ninth of May is still unused, and what was left was the plain right to put a question to a woman, which is the only one of the three that nobody else in that house has.**

### THREE FACTS BATCH 0005 ESTABLISHES THAT NO LATER PHASE MAY RE-DERIVE

1. **THE SECOND LINE ON THE WALL IS *UNRECORDED — CARRIED BY*, PAINTED ON THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER AT ABOUT SIX IN THE MORNING, IN THE OFFICE'S OWN HAND, AT THE HEIGHT OF A NAME ON A PLATE, UNDER THE LAST TILE, AND IT IS STILL THERE ON THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JANUARY.** The words came off the standing sheet of the first of October and into the two boxes at the foot of an ordinary repainting form by a clerk who was given the sheet and no instruction. **Agnete Lindqvist says it is the Church's four words with the hand cut off and REFUSES TO CLAIM IT, and that refusal is in the record.** No character in 491–500 says what it means.
2. **THE BALANCE OF THE ACCOUNT IS TWO HUNDRED AND ELEVEN THOUSAND, EIGHT HUNDRED AND FORTY GRAINS, AND IT IS NOT ZERO, AND 500 IS THE ONLY CHAPTER IN THE BOOK THAT MAY PRINT IT.** It is about one in seven of what went out on the first of October. The four days ended because the man emptying it had four days in him. The books did not move on the sixteenth of November and open once in the year and not twice, so the account cannot be closed, and it is carried forward four times a year by Hanneke Ruis out of her own head with no certificate behind it.
3. **THE HOUSEHOLD IS DISPERSED AND INTACT AND THE PROTECTION IS GONE.** Nine sworn women; the roll binds that house by the highest rank in any of its members; that was nine on the second of April and has been one since the first of October, so the binding has gone down with it and nothing before the thirtieth of December can put it back. **THREE OF THEM NEVER LIVE WITH HIM AGAIN — PETRA ILYIN OVER A LAUNDRY OFF CORDWAIN SINCE THE SECOND OF OCTOBER, NADIA FERROUS ON NIGHTS AT A BRANCH OFF A THIRD COURT SINCE THE ELEVENTH, AND ISOLDE MARCHETTI OVER THE BELLHOUSE STAGE SINCE THE ELEVENTH — AND ALL THREE OF THEM ARE ON THE DECEMBER ROTA AT THAT BRANCH AND COME IN ON THE NIGHT SHIFT ON THE FIRST OF JANUARY. NONE OF THE THREE HAS SAID WHY.**

**`PC-102` IS INHERITED AND IS NOT UNDONE, AND 486 IS WHERE IT STOPS HIM IN PUBLIC.** He tells four hundred people at about a quarter past five on the evening of the first of October that he cannot empty the account, because on the fourteenth of August three women in a kitchen drew against it deliberately and under the law of a standing household the head of that household may never empty it again. **THE HOUSEHOLD DECIDED ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF AUGUST, EIGHT TO ONE, AGNETE LINDQVIST AGAINST ON THE CHURCH'S OWN GROUND, AND IT COULD NOT BE REPRESENTED BY HIM AND WAS NOT. EIGHT WOMEN PUT THEIR HANDS ON THE STONE UNDER THE CARD AND HE PUT HIS IN HIS COAT. THAT IS NOT A SOFTENING OF `PC-102`. IT IS `PC-102` DOING EXACTLY WHAT IT WAS WRITTEN TO DO, AND IT IS WHY 486 CAN BE THE CLIMAX WITH THE ACCOUNT STILL FULL ON THE PAGE.**

**THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS 486 AND IT IS HIS AND IT CLOSES FOUR THINGS AT ONCE.** Roughly one million four hundred thousand grains out, in the open, at a wall, with his name on it. Rank nine to rank one, lodged at a public counter at ten minutes to four in his own hand in a queue of nine people with a pen, ninety seconds, no instrument, no witness, no holder, no receipt, no copy. Every permission he has ever bought, gone in the same hour. **And the file permanently closed at ten past four, on the same page, in the same session, by an amendment that carries his brother's name read once and entered once, and that Ferreira signs herself while saying she has not withdrawn one word of her case.**

### THREE FACTS BATCH 0003 ESTABLISHED THAT A LATER PHASE MUST NOT RE-DERIVE

1. **THE THREE WHO HAVE DRAWN AGAINST THE ACCOUNT ARE PETRA ILYIN, NADIA FERROUS AND MARGUERITE VALE, AND ALL THREE OF THEM DREW ON PURPOSE, IN THE OPEN, AT HALF PAST ELEVEN IN THE MORNING ON THE FOURTEENTH OF AUGUST, IN FRONT OF NINE PEOPLE.** Petra's is eleven years and unknowing and under somebody else's promise. Nadia's is about forty times over two years and she chose it every time for somebody who came up a shaft and could not climb. Marguerite's is eleven years and deliberate and she has never told anybody and **thinks she was taking it out of other people's doorways to keep one woman warm.** **THE THREE OF THEM ARE ON THE PAGE IN THAT ORDER AND NOT IN ANOTHER, AND 476 IS THE CHAPTER.**
2. **HE CANNOT EMPTY THE ACCOUNT ALONE AGAIN, EVER, AND THE THREE WOMEN WHO DID IT ARE THE REASON.** Under `PC-102` the moment any person keeping premises 34-6-118 has drawn against it, the head of that household may not empty it. **He refused to rescue them, refused to be the instrument, went out on the landing, and three women took the cold minutes themselves, and he says on the page that he did not arrange it, cannot reverse it, and there is no version of the next four weeks in which he is the one doing anything.**
3. **THE HOUSEHOLD HAS DECIDED AND HE IS ONE OF NINE.** On the twenty-sixth of August, at about nine in the evening, after telling nine women about his chest, after refusing the chair and going to the window, **nine women decided by hands on a tally kept on the back of a docket, eight to one, with Agnete Lindqvist against on the Church's own ground and in her own words.** **HE CAST THE NINTH VOTE AND IT WAS AGAINST THEM AND IT CHANGED NOTHING.** What the household decided is what will be said at the end of a corridor on the first of October and who will say it, **and Beatrix Halm has said out loud that it cannot be him, because a person keeping an address so named may be produced by a minute about that address only in the presence of the subject and the subject may not be absent from the making of it.** **HIS OWN CLAUSE OF THE TWENTY-FIRST OF JUNE IS WHAT PUTS HIM IN THE CORRIDOR OUTSIDE HIS OWN HOUSEHOLD'S DECISION, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS HE WOULD WRITE IT AGAIN.**

THE NOTICE THAT CALLS IT CARRIES A NUMBER, A DATE AND NOT ONE WORD, AND THE ONLY WAY TO TAKE A NOTICE OFF THE RANKS WALL IS TO GO AND ASK THE OFFICE THAT PUT IT UP. **A HEARING RECORD IS NOT A FINDING:** IT CARRIES NO SEAL, IT CANNOT BE ORDERED, IT CANNOT BE CLASSIFIED, IT CANNOT BE READ AT A VALUATION AND IT CANNOT BE STRUCK, **AND IT GOES UP ON THE STANDING SHEET AT THE HEAD OF EVERY LINE AND ANY PERSON IN THIS CITY MAY GO TO THE BUILDING, PUT THEIR NAME IN A DAY'S BOOK AND ASK TO READ IT.** A CLAIM ENTERED IN ONE MUST BE ANSWERED IN A ROOM ANYBODY MAY ATTEND, AND THE ANSWER IS A SEPARATE SITTING AND **NOT** A FIFTH DAY. | **461, 463, 468, 469** | **PROPOSED BY BATCH 0002 AND ON THE PAGE.** A clerk of the Correction Office reads the call out of a ladder against the stone at the north end of the Reading on the morning of the nineteenth of July, and a woman with a shopping bag asks the man on the step what it is about and he does not tell her, **and that refusal is the first real thing he does against the hearing and it buys three days.** He tries for September and is told the days are the days; a man from the Vestry tries for the order of the day and is told the same thing in a different tone. 1. **THE WALL SPAN IS NOW ONE FIGURE AND IT IS FIXED ON THE PAGE AT 461.** **He stood at the foot of the ranks wall on the eleventh, twelfth, thirteenth, fourteenth, fifteenth, sixteenth, seventeenth, eighteenth and nineteenth of July — nine days — and on no day after the nineteenth.** `461:3` and `461:129` are the anchor and were already correct: *been on the step … for eight days and the nineteenth of July was the ninth*, and *a wall he had been sitting under for nine days*. **`460` is seven days on the seventeenth, `465`, `469` and `470` say nine, `468` says *since the nineteenth* and *nine days*, and `470` prints the span with its operands.** **THE COUNT IS INCLUSIVE OF THE ELEVENTH AND NO LATER PHASE MAY RE-DERIVE IT FROM ANY OTHER DATE — NOT FROM THE FIFTEENTH, WHERE ADA'S FIVE DAYS IS CORRECT AND IS A DIFFERENT SPAN.** **WHY IT MATTERS AND WHY A REPAIR PASS OWES IT: 470 IS THE VOLUME'S PAYOFF AND THE ONE SENTENCE HE GIVES THERE IS A REPORT ON HIMSELF ENTERED IN A PUBLIC RECORD, AND A RECORD LIKE THAT HAS TO BE TRUE.**

### THREE FACTS BATCH 0002 ESTABLISHED THAT A LATER PHASE MUST NOT RE-DERIVE

1. **THE CLAIM IS IN THE RECORD AND IT IS THE CHURCH'S, NOT HIS TO ANSWER.** Three hundred and ninety words, entered on the twenty-ninth of July under the Church of the Quiet Number's own name, built out of two hours and fifty minutes of other people's private lives given freely in a room where nothing could be compelled. **He has answered it on the thirtieth in a room anybody may attend, without a document, without a rank and without an authority, and the answer conceded that it has never been his, and the answer did not carry the room.** The disposition is not for a hearing to decide and is not for any room before the first of October.
2. **TWO DOCUMENTS ARE ON THE STANDING SHEET AND NEITHER OF THEM HAS HIS NAME IN THE HEADING.** The claim of the twenty-ninth and the answer of the thirtieth, plus the four lines and an hour of the twenty-eighth kept on a shelf. **They go out at four in the afternoon to a room that stands at five and cannot sit again until the first of October.** On the first of October there will be nine seals, a claim, an answer that conceded, and a man who said yes to a classification he has not paid for.
3. **THE COST OF THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS ALREADY PAID AND IT IS NOT A WOUND.** He let somebody else pay for a true sentence. **What closes is the option of buying his way out of another person's ruin, and he will need it in October and will not have it, and the chapter says so on the page.** Sowe's standing, not his, is what went. **Sowe is not forgiven, by him or by Sowe, and no character in this batch uses the word, and the fixing of it is 498 and is not discussed.**

THE SPAN IS THIRTY-NINE AND FORTY.** Months in this book are thirty days. **28 September → 7 July is 2 + 270 + 7 = 279 days = thirty-nine weeks and six days, and 28 September → 13 July is 285 days = forty weeks and five days.** So: **455's *twenty-nine weeks* IS THIRTY-NINE at 119 AND 141**, and **458's *nine weeks* IS FORTY at 73 AND 77**, with **159's *three weeks missing out of my head* now *out of forty***, and **460:131's *nine weeks of a figure* IS FORTY.** **A LATER PHASE MUST NOT RE-DERIVE THESE OFF THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF SEPTEMBER AND LAND ON THE FIRST PASS FIGURES AGAIN; THE ANCHOR IS ON THE PAGE AT 455:97 AND 458:77.**
